床边检测与重组酶聚合酶扩增

Point of Care Testing and Recombinase Polymerase Amplification
作者： Manfred Weidmann, Ahmed Abd El Wahed, Weidmann M, El Wahed AA.
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨重组酶聚合酶扩增(RPA)作为床边检测(POCT)诊断工具的潜力,特别是在与其他等温扩增方法如LAMP的比较中,并解决在传染病领域实现灵敏且可扩展的POCT解决方案的挑战。
设计与对象
本研究回顾了现有文献,分析了RPA及相关等温扩增方法在床边检测(POCT)背景下的设计、性能和应用,重点关注其灵敏度、多重扩增能力以及环境影响。还考察了针对多种病原体(包括SARS-CoV-2)的POCT设备开发情况。
核心方法
本研究评估了RPA和LAMP检测方法中使用的手段,包括其灵敏度、多重扩增策略以及POCT设备中电子元件的应用。还基于现有文献考虑了这些设备的成本和环境影响。
主要结果
本研究发现,RPA和LAMP的灵敏度与实时PCR相当,但RT-RPA(实时RPA)有时在灵敏度上存在困难。还注意到,像minoo、CPod和Pebble这样的简单多用途POCT设备已经出现,其中minoo设备已验证可运行至少1000次。
解释与创新
作者认为,RPA正逐渐成为一种可行的POCT选项,尤其是在亚洲地区。开发简单、成本效益高且环保的设备可能有助于解决诊断可及性中的最后一公里问题。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,RPA可能是POCT的有力工具,特别是对于像猴痘这样的病原体,设备设计的进展可能提高低资源环境下的诊断可及性。
局限与证据强度
本研究的局限性在于其依赖现有文献,且缺乏对RPA在猴痘病原体上的直接实验数据。证据强度中等,因为其综合了多个研究的结果,而非提供原始数据。

Point of Care Testing and Recombinase Polymerase Amplification

Authors: Manfred Weidmann, Ahmed Abd El Wahed, Weidmann M, El Wahed AA.
Original abstract (English)
In the first decade of the 2000s, several isothermal molecular amplification methods emerged [1] but real‐time RPA stood out at the time as its design format was reminiscent of real‐time PCR (two primers and a probe). Dear Editor, Recombinase Polymerase Amplification (RPA) is coming of age and is poised to develop into a serious candidate for Point‐of‐care diagnostics. RPA has been around for almost 20 years. Originally developed by academics Nial Armes and Olaf Piepenburg, it was first published in 2006, and a first patent was issued in 2007. The probe used to confirm specific amplification was intuitively easier to accept than the backfolding FIP/BIB primers of Loop Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP), which in essence do the same. To this day, this misunderstanding of the LAMP mechanism still leads to the development of additional real‐time probe system for LAMP in order to add a confirmatory probe [2, 3, 4, 5]. This may, however, also be the case because of the well‐documented unspecific amplification issues of LAMP [6]. In terms of sensitivity, both LAMP and RPA have been shown to be as sensitive as real‐time PCR in numerous studies; RT‐RPA, however, sometimes struggles in the last decade of sensitivity. An intriguing recent analysis speculates that, in contrast to fully biphasic UvsX‐GP32 droplets formed in the RPA reaction, the addition of RT leads to fully miscible droplets including BsuI, which inhibits UvsX and thus may cause the loss of sensitivity in RT‐RPA [7]. Both LAMP and RPA struggle with one tube multiplexing but the introduction of cascade amplification using CRISPR/Cas12 and 13 systems which digest ssDNA or ssRNA reports offers solutions but the most promising appear to be spatial multiplexing concepts on surfaces or digital droplets. From the...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore the potential of Recombinase Polymerase Amplification (RPA) as a point-of-care (POCT) diagnostic tool, particularly in comparison to other isothermal amplification methods like LAMP, and to address the challenges in achieving sensitive and scalable POCT solutions for infectious diseases.
Design and population
The study reviewed existing literature and analyzed the design, performance, and application of RPA and related isothermal amplification methods in the context of POCT, focusing on their sensitivity, multiplexing capabilities, and environmental impact. It also examined the development of POCT devices for various pathogens, including SARS-CoV-2.
Core methods
The study evaluated the methods used in RPA and LAMP assays, including their sensitivity, multiplexing strategies, and the use of electronic components in POCT devices. It also considered the cost of goods for these devices and their environmental impact based on available literature.
Main results
The study found that RPA and LAMP have comparable sensitivity to real-time PCR, but RT-RPA sometimes struggles with sensitivity. It also noted that simple multi-use POCT devices like minoo, CPod, and Pebble have emerged, with the minoo device verified for at least 1000 runs.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret that RPA is gaining traction as a viable POCT option, especially in Asia, and that the development of simple, cost-effective, and environmentally friendly devices could address the last-mile problem in diagnostics access.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that RPA could be a promising tool for POCT, particularly for pathogens like mpox, and that advancements in device design may improve diagnostic accessibility in low-resource settings.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on existing literature and the lack of direct experimental data on RPA's performance for mpox. The evidence is moderate, as it synthesizes findings from multiple studies rather than presenting original data.

新发传染病(猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯山脉):传播、环境生存和消毒的叙述性回顾

Emerging infectious diseases (Mpox, Nipah, and Andes): A narrative review of transmission, environmental survival, and disinfection
作者： David J. Weber, William A. Rutala, April Baller, Brooke E Brewer, Emily E. Sickbert-Bennett
摘要中文翻译背景:猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒是新出现的传染病,导致与医院 传播 相关的 暴发。方法:这篇叙述性综述总结了 猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒 流行病学、临床表现、诊断 和 治疗,重点是环境生存、对防腐剂和消毒剂的敏感性以及感染预防。结果:猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒都可以在环境中生存,并且能够间接感染 传播。所有这些病毒都有包膜,对常用的防腐剂和表面消毒剂敏感。结论:快速识别已知或疑似感染这些新出现病毒的患者,并遵守感染预防建议,将预防与医疗保健相关的 传播。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
猴痘、Nipah病毒和Andes病毒是引起传播的新兴传染病,与医院获得性传播(A1)相关。
证据基础与方法
本叙述性综述总结了猴痘、Nipah病毒和Andes病毒的流行病学、临床表现、诊断和治疗,重点包括环境存活能力、对常用消毒剂和抗菌剂的敏感性以及感染预防(A2).
共识与结论
猴痘、Nipah病毒和Andes病毒能够在环境中存活,并且是包膜病毒,因此对常用抗菌剂和消毒剂敏感(A3,A4).
争议与缺口
提供的证据中未报告任何争议或证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
应优先快速识别疑似感染的患者,并严格执行感染预防建议,以防止医院相关的传播(A5)。

Emerging infectious diseases (Mpox, Nipah, and Andes): A narrative review of transmission, environmental survival, and disinfection

Authors: David J. Weber, William A. Rutala, April Baller, Brooke E Brewer, Emily E. Sickbert-Bennett
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus are emerging infectious diseases that cause outbreaks associated with nosocomial transmission. METHODS: This narrative review summarizes the Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus epidemiology, clinical manifestations, diagnosis, and treatment with a focus on environmental survival, susceptibility to antiseptics and disinfectants, and infection prevention. RESULTS: Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus all survive in the environment and are capable of indirect transmission. All these viruses are enveloped and susceptible to commonly used antiseptics and surface disinfectants. CONCLUSIONS: Rapid identification of patients with known or suspected infections of these emerging viruses coupled with adherence to infection prevention recommendations will prevent healthcare-associated transmission.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus are emerging infectious diseases that cause outbreaks associated with nosocomial transmission (A1).
Evidence base and method
This narrative review summarizes the Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus epidemiology, clinical manifestations, diagnosis, and treatment with a focus on environmental survival, susceptibility to antiseptics and disinfectants, and infection prevention (A2).
Consensus and conclusions
Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus survive in the environment and are enveloped, making them susceptible to commonly used antiseptics and disinfectants (A3, A4).
Controversies and gaps
No controversies or evidence gaps are reported in the supplied evidence.
Research and practice implications
Prioritize rapid identification of patients with suspected infections and enforce infection prevention recommendations to prevent healthcare-associated transmission (A5).

猴痘病毒对策:疫苗、抗体、药物和中药

Monkeypox Virus Countermeasures: Vaccines, Antibodies, Drugs and Traditional Chinese Medicine
作者： Xinrui Xu, Yubo Nie, Yanliang Zhang, Jing Liu, 聂宇波
摘要中文翻译摘要: 猴痘病毒(MPXV)近年来引起了全球的关注。MPXV 是一种 DNA 病毒,属于 正痘病毒,与天花病毒 (VARV) 和痘苗病毒 (VACV) 表现出密切的遗传和结构相似性。尽管世界卫生组织(WHO)于2023年宣布全球猴痘病毒(猴痘)不再构成公共卫生紧急事件,但大多数地区报告的新病例显示了开发针对猴痘药物的紧迫性。在这篇综述中,我们回顾了有关猴痘的大量文献,总结了其发展、科学发现以及病毒结构、结合靶点、治疗和干预策略方面的可靠数据。由于美国食品和药物管理局(FDA)或世界卫生组织尚未正式授权抗病毒药物药物用于猴痘的靶向治疗,再加上当前抗病毒药物药物在临床评估中证明的有效性有限,耐药性的逐渐出现以及新型病毒变异体带来的持续威胁,猴痘的新抗病毒药物疗法的进展已经出现全球公共卫生领域的一个紧迫优先事项。目的是探索猴痘的治疗措施、治疗技术和药物开发流程,包括疫苗、抗体、化学物质、天然药物单体、中药和中药制剂。此外,它还清楚地区分了 猴痘 疗法开发中已批准药物、临床前候选化合物和计算机预测分子的进展情况。有利于了解MPXV的研究基础,提高对预防措施和治疗方法的认识,促进医疗保健服务。关键词:正痘病毒,中医,治疗发展
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
审查的重点是猴痘病毒,检查疫苗、抗体、药物和中药(TCM)作为对策。核心问题是 猴痘 治疗干预措施的开发和评估,包括其有效性、安全性和临床应用潜力。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 猴痘 大量文献的综合分析,总结了与病毒结构、结合靶点、治疗 和干预策略相关的发现。它包括 猴痘 疗法开发中已批准药物、临床前候选化合物和计算机预测分子的详细区分。
共识与结论
尽管世界卫生组织宣布2023年全球猴痘 暴发不再是突发公共卫生事件,但大多数地区的新事件凸显了对有效药物的迫切需求。该综述强调了了解 猴痘病毒 生物学和开发新 抗病毒药物 疗法的重要性。
争议与缺口
审查指出,FDA 或 WHO 尚未正式授权 抗病毒药物 药物用于靶向 猴痘 治疗,并且目前的 抗病毒药物 药物在临床评估中显示效果有限。此外,耐药性的逐渐出现和新型病毒变种的持续威胁被认为是尚未解决的挑战。
科研与实践启示
鉴于缺乏批准的 治疗 以及耐药性的出现,该审查强调了推进 猴痘 新 抗病毒药物 疗法的重要性。它还强调需要提高对预防措施和治疗方法的理解,以加强医疗保健服务。

Monkeypox Virus Countermeasures: Vaccines, Antibodies, Drugs and Traditional Chinese Medicine

Authors: Xinrui Xu, Yubo Nie, Yanliang Zhang, Jing Liu, 聂宇波
Original abstract (English)
Abstract: Monkeypox virus (MPXV) has garnered global attention in recent years. MPXV is a DNA virus that is categorized as belonging to the Orthopoxvirus, exhibiting close genetic and structural similarities to variola virus (VARV) and vaccinia virus (VACV). Despite the World Health Organization (WHO) announcing in 2023 that the global outbreak of monkeypox (mpox) is no longer a public health emergency, the new occurrence reported in most regions shows the urgency of the development of medicines for mpox. In this review, we reviewed a huge amount of literature on mpox, summarizing its development, scientific findings, and trustworthy data when it comes to viral structure, binding targets, treatments, and intervention strategies. Because no antiviral agents have been formally authorized by the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) or the WHO for the targeted treatment of mpox, coupled with the limited effectiveness demonstrated by current antiviral medications in clinical evaluations, the progressive emergence of drug resistance, and the ongoing threat posed by novel viral variants, the advancement of new antiviral therapeutics for mpox has emerged as an urgent priority within the domain of global public health. The purpose is to explore measures, therapy techniques, and the medication development process for mpox, including vaccines, antibodies, chemicals, natural drug monomers, traditional Chinese medicines (TCM), and Chinese herbal formulations. Also, it clearly differentiates the progress of approved drugs, preclinical candidate compounds, and computer-predicted molecules in the development of therapeutics for mpox. It is beneficial to comprehend MPXV’s research basis, improve the understanding of preventive measures and treatment methods, and promote medical and healthcare services. Keywords: orthopoxvirus, traditional Chinese medicine, therapeutic development
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the Mpox virus, examining vaccines, antibodies, drugs, and traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) as countermeasures. The central question is the development and evaluation of therapeutic interventions for mpox, including their efficacy, safety, and potential for clinical application.
Evidence base and method
The review is a comprehensive analysis of a large body of literature on mpox, summarizing findings related to viral structure, binding targets, treatments, and intervention strategies. It includes a detailed differentiation of approved drugs, preclinical candidate compounds, and computer-predicted molecules in the development of mpox therapeutics.
Consensus and conclusions
Despite the WHO declaring the global mpox outbreak no longer a public health emergency in 2023, new occurrences in most regions highlight the urgent need for effective medicines. The review emphasizes the importance of understanding the Mpox virus's biology and the development of new antiviral therapeutics.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that no antiviral agents have been formally authorized by the FDA or WHO for targeted mpox treatment, and current antiviral medications show limited effectiveness in clinical evaluations. Additionally, the progressive emergence of drug resistance and the ongoing threat from novel viral variants are identified as unresolved challenges.
Research and practice implications
The review underscores the importance of advancing new antiviral therapeutics for mpox, given the lack of approved treatments and the emergence of drug resistance. It also highlights the need for improved understanding of preventive measures and treatment methods to enhance medical and healthcare services.

公共卫生紧急情况下的风险沟通策略:关于框架、近期危机和新兴挑战的叙述性综述

Risk communication strategies during public health emergencies: a narrative review of frameworks, recent crises, and emerging challenges
作者： Alexandre Bueno Merlini, Alexandre Biasi Cavalcanti, Daniel Apolinário, Renan Henrique Aparecido Camilo Merlini, Priscila Rosseto de Toledo
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于公共卫生紧急情况下的风险沟通策略,特别是对猴痘病毒及其相关疾病进行探讨。核心问题是:在暴发期间如何实现有效的沟通,特别强调透明度、公平性和信息过载管理。
证据基础与方法
The review is a narrative synthesis based on an iterative search of MEDLINE/PubMed, Embase, Google Scholar, and grey literature from major public health agencies, covering the period from 2018 to 2026. It includes evidence from recent crises such as the pre-vaccine phase of the COVID-19 pandemic, the 2022 and 2024 mpox outbreaks, and the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak.
共识与结论
综述强调,风险沟通是公共卫生紧急响应的重要组成部分,不能脱离其所属的协调应急响应系统单独评估。它强调需要一种贯穿整个应急响应系统的横向功能,以支持所有应急响应方面,包括社区参与和确保信息传播的公平性。
争议与缺口
风险沟通框架主要针对高收入国家,且干预措施效果的系统性评估较为缺乏。此外,沟通策略中对不确定性的透明度不足,往往更注重权威形象而非诚实对话。
科研与实践启示
未来研究应优先考虑在低收入和中等收入国家开展社区参与的干预措施,加强与行为流行病学和公共卫生伦理的交叉融合,并开发能够衡量沟通结果公平性和质量的指标。

Risk communication strategies during public health emergencies: a narrative review of frameworks, recent crises, and emerging challenges

Authors: Alexandre Bueno Merlini, Alexandre Biasi Cavalcanti, Daniel Apolinário, Renan Henrique Aparecido Camilo Merlini, Priscila Rosseto de Toledo
Original abstract (English)
Public health emergencies, ranging from infectious disease outbreaks to climate-driven and humanitarian crises, generate communication demands that conventional health messaging often fails to meet. Risk communication is increasingly recognized as a determinant of population behavior, healthcare system burden, and ultimately morbidity and mortality during crises. The COVID-19 pandemic exposed substantial limitations in existing approaches, particularly regarding transparency, equity, and the management of information overload, and major retrospective assessments have characterized the global response as a systemic failure across multiple coordinated domains, of which communication is one. This narrative review synthesizes evidence on risk communication during public health emergencies, based on an iterative search of MEDLINE/PubMed, Embase, Google Scholar, and grey literature from major public health agencies covering 2018 to 2026. The synthesis is organized around three axes. Established frameworks are compared, including Crisis and Emergency Risk Communication, the World Health Organization guidance on emergency risk communication, and emerging community-centered and equity-focused models, with attention to how they complement, overlap with, and diverge from one another. Lessons are then drawn from recent crises, including the pre-vaccine phase of the COVID-19 pandemic, the 2022 and 2024 mpox outbreaks, and the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Uganda. Persistent challenges are examined last, encompassing the infodemic, erosion of institutional trust, inequities in message reach, the dual role of digital, social, and traditional media, and the behavioral responses mediating the effects of messaging. Risk communication cannot be evaluated in isolation from the coordinated emergency response of which it forms a part, and is better understood as a transversal function serving every component of that response. Frameworks remain heavily centered on high-income contexts, transparency about uncertainty is often sacrificed for the appearance of authority, and systematic evaluation of intervention effectiveness is scarce. Emergency departments represent one important operational interface among several coordinated levels of the response rather than the sole communication setting. Future work should prioritize community-engaged interventions in low- and middle-income settings, cross-fertilization with behavioral epidemiology and public health ethics, and metrics capturing both the equity and the quality of communication outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on risk communication strategies during public health emergencies, particularly examining the Mpox virus and its associated disease. The central question is how effective communication can be during outbreaks, with an emphasis on transparency, equity, and managing information overload.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis based on an iterative search of MEDLINE/PubMed, Embase, Google Scholar, and grey literature from major public health agencies, covering the period from 2018 to 2026. It includes evidence from recent crises such as the pre-vaccine phase of the COVID-19 pandemic, the 2022 and 2024 mpox outbreaks, and the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that risk communication is a critical component of public health emergency responses and cannot be evaluated in isolation. It emphasizes the need for transversal functions that support all aspects of the emergency response, including the importance of community engagement and addressing equity in message reach.
Controversies and gaps
Frameworks for risk communication are heavily centered on high-income contexts, and systematic evaluation of intervention effectiveness is scarce. Additionally, there is a lack of transparency about uncertainty in communication strategies, often prioritizing the appearance of authority over honest dialogue.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize community-engaged interventions in low- and middle-income settings, cross-fertilization with behavioral epidemiology and public health ethics, and the development of metrics that capture both the equity and quality of communication outcomes.

Global initiative launched to improve access to mpox vaccines - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) launched a global initiative on 2 September 2026 to improve access to mpox vaccines, aiming to address vaccine shortages and enhance public health preparedness. This initiative is part of broader efforts to combat mpox disease, which is caused by the monkeypox virus (Orthopoxvirus monkeypox). The evidence highlights the WHO's commitment to ensuring equitable vaccine distribution, but no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers are reported. The initiative is confirmed as an official public health action, with no further details on testing, containment, or risk assessment provided. The event is described as a global effort, targeting populations affected by mpox across multiple countries.

猴痘(猴痘):症状、传播方式及预防措施——全国老年事务委员会(NCOA)

Mpox (Monkeypox): Symptoms, How It Spreads, and Prevention - The National Council on Aging (NCOA)
新闻简报全国老年事务委员会(NCOA)于2026年9月2日发布的一篇文章提供了关于猴痘(猴痘)的一般信息,包括其症状、传播、预防措施以及老年人的风险因素。这是一篇指导性文章,而非暴发报告。文章指出,猴痘是一种由猴痘病毒引起的病毒性疾病,可通过与感染者或动物的密切接触传播。常见症状包括皮疹、发热、寒战、淋巴结肿大、头痛和身体疼痛,大多数患者在2–4周内康复。文章提到,尽管对一般公众的风险仍然较低,但近期病例与前往暴发地区或与感染者密切接触有关。它还强调,I型毒株与更严重的疾病和更高的死亡率相关,而II型毒株通常引起较轻的症状。截至2024年6月12日,全球已报告超过97,000例确认的猴痘病例。文章指出,某些免疫状况或风险因素较高的人群,如有多名性伴侣或HIV感染者,感染风险更高。它建议高风险人群进行疫苗接种,并建议在暴露后及时寻求医疗指导。文章未提供当前调查状态或防控措施的具体细节。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox (Monkeypox): Symptoms, How It Spreads, and Prevention - The National Council on Aging (NCOA)

News Brief
The article from The National Council on Aging (NCOA) published on September 2, 2026, provides general information about mpox (monkeypox), including its symptoms, transmission, prevention, and risk factors for older adults. It is a guidance article, not an outbreak report. The text explains that mpox is a viral disease caused by the monkeypox virus, which can spread through close contact with an infected person or animal. Common symptoms include a rash, fever, chills, swollen lymph nodes, headache, and body aches, with most people recovering within 2–4 weeks. The article notes that while the risk to the general public remains low, recent cases have been linked to travel to regions with outbreaks or close contact with infected individuals. It also highlights that clade I is associated with more severe illness and higher mortality, while clade II generally causes less severe symptoms. As of June 12, 2024, over 97,000 confirmed mpox cases were reported globally. The article emphasizes that people with certain immune conditions or risk factors, such as multiple sexual partners or HIV, are at higher risk. It recommends vaccination for high-risk individuals and advises seeking medical guidance if exposed. The text does not provide specific details on current investigation status or containment measures.
Original source text (English)

For more information on what this means and how we use your data, please see our Privacy Policy. Close What Is Monkeypox (Mpox)? What Older Adults Need to Know Physical Health Sep 02, 2026 11 min read Print Page Share Related Topics Prevention Physical Health Monkeypox, now officially called mpox, is a viral disease caused by the monkeypox virus. It can spread from person to person and typically causes a rash and other flu-like symptoms. Mpox is sometimes confused with shingles because both illnesses cause painful skin bumps and fluid-filled blisters. While the risk of mpox to the general public remains low, recent cases have been linked with travel to regions where outbreaks are occurring or close contact with someone who has mpox. Here’s what you need to know about the mpox virus, symptoms of mpox illness, how it spreads, and who is most at risk. Mpox Quick Facts What is mpox? A contagious illness caused by the monkeypox virus How does it spread? Mainly through close contact with someone who has mpox Common symptoms Rash, fever, chills, swollen lymph nodes, headache, and body aches How long does it last? Most people recover within 2–4 weeks Who is at higher risk? People with certain immune conditions and other risk factors Is it serious? Most cases are mild, but serious illness can occur Is there a vaccine? Yes, vaccination is recommended for certain people at higher risk What if I'm exposed? Contact a health care provider promptly for guidance What is the history of mpox (monkeypox)? The mpox virus was discovered in a Denmark laboratory in 1958 when several laboratory monkeys developed a pox-like rash. The first report of a human contracting mpox was in 1970, in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. Public health officials say the disease is endemic in some central and west African countries, and prior to the 2022 outbreak, mpox cases in the United States were “linked to international travel to countries where the disease commonly occurs or through imported animals.” What's the difference between clade I and clade II mpox? There are two main types of the monkeypox virus, known as clade I and clade II. A clade refers to a group of closely related viruses. Clade I has historically circulated in Central Africa and is generally associated with more severe illness and a higher risk of death. It may also spread more easily. Clade II has historically circulated in West Africa and generally causes less severe illness and fewer deaths than clade I. A subtype of clade II caused the global mpox outbreak that began in 2022. Is there an mpox outbreak in the U.S.? Not currently. But there was a rare mpox outbreak back in 2022. On May 18, 2022, the United States recorded the year’s first case of mpox. By late September, while older adults were rolling up their sleeves for an updated COVID-19 vaccine, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) had tallied 24,572 confirmed mpox cases. Since the peak of the mpox outbreak in 2022, the highest monthly case counts occurred in the fall of 2025. Today, clade II mpox is still circulating at low levels nationally, and cases of clade I are rising as of July 2026 according to CDC. How does mpox spread? Mpox is transmitted through “close, personal, often skin-to-skin contact,” CDC says. This includes direct contact with mpox rash, scabs, or body fluids from a person with mpox; touching objects, fabrics (clothing, bedding, or towels), and surfaces that have been used by someone with mpox; and contact with respiratory secretions. Mpox is zoonotic, which means animals can transmit it to people, but “monkeys are not a main source of transmission,” Yale University epidemiologist Saad Omer said in an interview with the Yale School of Medicine. “... it’s the rodents that we need to worry about in terms of actual transmission.” In the U.S. in 2003, there were 47 “confirmed and probable” mpox cases linked to contact with pet prairie dogs, who arrived in a shipment of mammals that included several types of rodents. What caused the 2022 mpox outbreak? Researchers discovered that during the 2022-2023 mpox outbreak, the Clade IIb genetic sub-lineage of the virus adapted to efficiently spread primarily through close, intimate physical contact, mainly within interconnected sexual networks. As of June 12, 2024, more than 97,000 confirmed mpox cases had been reported across 118 countries as part of the global outbreak that began in 2022.1 Is mpox life-threatening? Mpox is rarely fatal, and more than 99% of people who get it survive. But the risk of serious illness may be higher for people with weakened immune systems, young children, people with a history of eczema, and those who are pregnant or breastfeeding. When the Los Angeles, California, Department of Health reported the country’s first death to mpox in mid-September 2022, officials said the person who succumbed to the virus was “severely immunocompromised.” Is mpox dangerous for oldera dults? Through Sept. 22, 2022, demographic data show that confirmed mpox cases for adults ages 61 and older were among the lowest recorded. CDC reported 277 cases in that age group. CDC does not cite older age in itself as a risk factor for older adults, but public health officials stressed that anyone who’s had close, personal contact with someone who has mpox is at risk. Who is at highest risk for mpox? Following the 2022-2023 mpox outbreak, a review of 31 studies found that the risk of getting mpox was higher among people who had close contact with an infected person or animal, had multiple sexual partners or unprotected sex, or had HIV or another sexually transmitted infection. Men who have sex with men also had a higher risk of infection in the populations studied.2 What are the symptoms of mpox? A rash, sometimes painful, is the most common symptom of the mpox virus. It can appear and spread on or near the genitals or anus, or on other areas of the body like the hands, feet, chest, face, or mouth. The mpox rash goes through several stages, including scabs, before healing. Flu-like symptoms are other signs of infection. These can include: Fever Chills Swollen lymph nodes Exhaustion Muscle aches and backache Headache Respiratory symptoms. If you think you have symptoms or have been in close contact with someone who has the virus, talk to your doctor about whether you should be tested. If you have been exposed, watch for mpox symptoms for 21 days from the date of exposure. How do you prevent mpox? You can help protect yourself and others by following these CDC-provided guidelines: Avoid close, skin-to-skin contact with people who have a rash that looks like mpox. Do not touch the rash or scabs of a person with mpox. Do not kiss, hug, cuddle, or have sex with someone with mpox. Avoid contact with objects and materials that a person with mpox has used. Don’t share eating utensils or cups with a person with mpox. Don’t handle or touch the bedding, towels, or clothing of a person with mpox. Wash your hands often with soap and water or use an alcohol-based hand sanitizer, especially before eating or touching your face and after you use the bathroom. What if I get mpox? “Currently there is no Food and Drug Administration (FDA)-approved treatment specifically for monkeypox,” CDC says. “For most patients with monkeypox who don't have severe disease or risk factors for severe disease (e.g., severe immunocompromise), supportive care and pain control will help them recover.” Even without a specific mpox treatment, there are a number of ways to manage your symptoms, including getting plenty of rest. Most people recover within 2-4 weeks. Should I get an mpox vaccine? CDC says, “If you have certain risk factors that make you eligible, you can help protect yourself from monkeypox by getting the 2-dose monkeypox vaccine.” Talk to your health care professional or local health department to learn more about JYNNEOS, the two-dose vaccine developed to protect against smallpox and mpox. How to stay informed about mpox You can keep up with mpox developments and guidance on the CDC website, a trusted source for information on topics ranging from mpox prevention to mpox treatment. Data is updated frequently. WHO offers a comprehensive FAQ and a global perspective. And as always, if you have any health concerns, talk to your doctor. You can also take steps to protect your overall health by staying up to date on your flu, COVID, and RSV vaccines. Learn more about the vaccines recommended for older adults. Sources 1. The article provides general information about mpox (monkeypox), including its symptoms, transmission, prevention, and risk factors for older adults. It is a guidance article, not an outbreak report.
Scale, impact and risk
As of June 12, 2024, more than 97,000 confirmed mpox cases had been reported globally. The risk of mpox is higher among people with close contact with infected individuals or animals, multiple sexual partners, unprotected sex, or HIV and other sexually transmitted infections. 猴痘新闻

Mpox News
新闻简报The news report highlights the global spread of the mpox virus, particularly the clade Ib variant, which has caused 17 deaths in Africa and 164 infections in Uganda. The UK confirmed a new mpox variant in a traveller from Asia, while Canada reported its first case of mpox clade I virus in a traveller returning from Africa. The WHO and Africa CDC have launched a 'Joint Continental Response Plan' to address the outbreak, starting in September 2024 and ending in February 2025. In response, the Indian government issued detailed guidelines for all states and Union Territories to prevent further spread. Mpox vaccination campaigns have begun in the Democratic Republic of Congo, with the first batch of 1 lakh doses arriving. The report also discusses the potential for mpox to cause a pandemic, similar to the previous COVID-19 outbreak, and the implementation of pandemic-like restrictions in airports and hospitals. These measures aim to control the spread and manage the health risks associated with the mpox virus.
查看新闻五要素
时间
英国于12月5日确认了一名来自亚洲的旅行者中出现的新猴痘变种,乌干达在最新报告中确认了其首例猴痘死亡病例,共有164例感染。世界卫生组织(WHO)于2024年9月启动了'联合大陆应对计划',并计划持续至2025年2月。
地点与对象
英国、加拿大、乌干达和印度被提及为受猴痘影响的地区。全球范围内,猴痘 Ib亚型变种正在传播,特别提到了非洲和刚果民主共和国。受影响的人群包括旅行者、医疗机构和一般公众。
事件
加拿大确认了一名来自非洲的旅行者中出现的首例猴痘 I型病毒病例。世界卫生组织(WHO)和非洲疾病预防控制中心(Africa CDC)启动了'联合大陆应对计划',以应对猴痘 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
The clade Ib mpox variant has caused 17 deaths in Africa and 164 infections in Uganda. The WHO has raised concerns about the global spread of mpox and its potential to cause a pandemic. Mpox vaccination campaigns have started in the Democratic Republic of Congo, with the first batch of 1 lakh doses arriving.
应对、状态与不确定性
英国当局正在监测新猴痘变种的传播情况。世界卫生组织(WHO)和非洲疾病预防控制中心(Africa CDC)已启动'联合大陆应对计划',以应对猴痘的应对工作。在刚果民主共和国,猴痘 疫苗接种宣传活动已开始。印度政府已发布指南,以遏制该国猴痘的传播。

新闻简报由UNICEF美国发布的文章,发表于2026年9月2日,讨论了非洲持续发生的猴痘 暴发疫情,特别是刚果民主共和国(DRC)。2024年的暴发疫情主要由I型变种引发,其中I型猴痘变种导致的感染更为严重,且死亡率高于II型。2024年1月至8月中旬期间,共有8,772名儿童感染,其中463人死亡。大多数死亡发生在儿童中,尤其是免疫系统较弱的儿童。世界卫生组织因该病毒传播至邻国而将其列为国际关注的公共卫生紧急事件。UNICEF正在与Gavi合作,以增加疫苗疫苗的可及性,并加强低收入和中等收入国家对暴发疫情的应对。猴痘 疫苗疫苗在预防严重疾病方面是安全且有效的,风险人群被鼓励接种疫苗。文章还概述了预防措施,包括避免与感染者接触以及使用个人防护装备。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-09-02。2024年的暴发疫情数据包括2024年1月至8月中旬期间的病例。
地点与对象
The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) is the epicenter of the 2024 mpox outbreak. Children under age 15 represent 56 percent of mpox cases reported in the country. The disease is also spreading to neighboring countries.
事件
一种新的猴痘变种,即猴痘病毒,正在非洲导致儿童死亡。刚果民主共和国首次报告人类猴痘病例是在1970年,一名9个月大的男孩。2024年的暴发疫情是由I型暴发变种引发的,与I型的两个变种Ia和Ib有关。
规模、影响与风险
Between January 2024 and mid-August 2024, 8,772 children were infected and 463 had died. From January 1, 2022, through July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed cases of mpox and 529 deaths. Most deaths occur in children, especially those weakened by malnutrition or other conditions.
应对、状态与不确定性
世界卫生组织因该病毒传播至多个国家而将其列为国际关注的公共卫生紧急事件。UNICEF正在支持Gavi和疫苗联盟的工作,以储备猴痘 疫苗疫苗,并加强低收入和中等收入国家对暴发疫情的应对。猴痘 疫苗疫苗在预防严重疾病方面是安全且高度有效的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox - unicef usa

News Brief
The article from UNICEF USA, published on September 2, 2026, discusses the ongoing mpox outbreak in Africa, particularly in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). The 2024 outbreak was driven by clade I variants, with clade I mpox causing more severe infections and higher mortality rates compared to clade II. Between January 2024 and mid-August 2024, 8,772 children were infected, and 463 had died. Most deaths occur in children, especially those with weakened immune systems. The World Health Organization declared mpox a public health emergency of international concern due to its spread to neighboring countries. UNICEF is collaborating with Gavi to increase vaccine access and strengthen outbreak response in low- and middle-income nations. The mpox vaccine is safe and effective at preventing severe disease, and those at risk are encouraged to get vaccinated. The article also outlines prevention measures, including avoiding physical contact with infected individuals and using personal protective equipment.

Breadcrumb Home What UNICEF Does Children's Health Mpox https://www.unicefusa.org/what-unicef-does/childrens-health/mpox A new variant of mpox, or monkeypox, is killing children in Africa. What causes this disease, and who is at risk? The answers to these and other frequently asked questions. Updated Sept. 2, 2026 What is mpox? Mpox, also known as monkeypox, is an infectious disease caused by an orthopoxvirus. It can spread from animals to people and from person to person. Cases of mpox are most often found in areas with tropical rainforests, home to squirrels, monkeys and other small mammals that are susceptible. The virus was discovered in 1958 in Denmark in monkeys kept for research. The first reported human case of mpox was in 1970, in a 9-month-old boy in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. There are two genetic clades of the virus, clade I and clade II. Clade I mpox tends to cause a higher number of severe infections and have a higher mortality rate than clade II mpox. Most deaths occur in children. Related: Children account for half of suspected mpox cases in Guinea-Bissau How prevalent is mpox? Mpox remains a significant and ongoing global health burden. From Jan. 1, 2022 through July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed cases of mpox and 529 deaths. Roughly two-thirds of reported cases were in Africa. In 2022, there was a global outbreak of clade II mpox, which led to more than 95,000 cases across 115 non-endemic countries. The 2024 outbreak was a clade I outbreak. An upsurge of mpox cases in the DRC that year was driven by outbreaks associated with two variants of clade I, clade Ia and clade Ib. What are the symptoms of mpox? Mpox causes symptoms that are similar to smallpox, though less severe. Symptoms include skin rash, fever, sore throat, headache, body aches, back pain, low energy and swollen lymph nodes. Some people who contract mpox get very sick. Most people fully recover within weeks. How is mpox spread? The mpox virus is spread through: contact with bodily fluids or skin lesions inhaling contaminated particles, such as respiratory droplets, from an infected person's mouth or throat contact with infected animals while hunting, skinning, cooking or eating contact with contaminated materials such as clothing, bedding and eating utensils Pregnant persons can pass the virus on to their unborn baby. Who is at risk of mpox? Anyone can get mpox. Children who get mpox are at higher risk of dying from the disease, especially children already weakened by malnutrition or other conditions. The DRC is the epicenter of the 2024 outbreak, with children under age 15 representing 56 percent of mpox cases reported in the country. Between January 2024 and mid-August 2024, 8,772 children were infected and 463 had died. With the DRC experiencing the largest number of suspected cases ever recorded in a single year, and with the disease spreading to several neighboring countries where it had not been found previously, the World Health Organization declared mpox a public health emergency of international concern on Aug. 14, 2024. For most people in the United States, the risk of clade 1 remains low, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. How is mpox diagnosed? To test for mpox, skin or fluid samples are taken directly from the rash. According to the World Health Organization, antigen and antibody detection methods may not be useful as they do not distinguish between orthopoxviruses. What is the best way to stay protected from mpox? Avoid face-to-face, skin-to-skin, mouth-to-skin and mouth-to-mouth contact with people who have been confirmed to have the virus or who have signs and symptoms of infection. Avoid touching or sharing personal items like bedding, eating utensils, clothes, phones or other belongings of a person who has the signs and symptoms. Avoid contact with all wild animals, alive or dead, especially those known to carry the virus, such as rodents or monkeys and those that appear sick or have been found dead. Wear personal protective equipment if you are caring for a person with mpox. Those who feel they are at risk are encouraged to get vaccinated with the mpox vaccine, which has been demonstrated to be safe and highly effective at preventing severe disease from mpox. UNICEF is a partner in efforts to funded by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, to increase access to vaccines and strengthen mpox outbreak response capacities globally. More on UNICEF's role below. Do I have to quarantine if I have mpox? It is advised to avoid physical contact with others and to stay home until scabs from lesions have fallen off and a new layer of skin has formed. If you must be around other people, cover all lesions and wear a well-fitting mask. How does UNICEF respond to mpox outbreaks? As with other infectious diseases that pose a threat to children and families in countries where UNICEF works, UNICEF takes a multi-faceted approach that includes: training and supporting volunteers to communicate risks to the public and to engage community members in prevention and control measures providing medical supplies and health worker training to build treatment capacity at health facilities distributing hygiene kits to families supporting vaccination campaigns to increase immunity promoting and enabling data sharing and other measures to improve monitoring and surveillance What is UNICEF's role in improving access to mpox vaccines? UNICEF is supporting accelerated efforts by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, to stockpile mpox vaccines and strengthen outbreak response in low- and middle-income countries. UNICEF is responsible for establishing, procuring for, and managing emergency vaccine stockpiles. Upon request from the International Coordinating Group (ICG) on Vaccine Provision, UNICEF manages the rapid delivery of stockpile vaccines and safe injection devices in response to disease outbreaks. UNICEF also supports governments and local partners to ensure that these supplies reach children and communities. Learn more about what UNICEF does to support children's health and to protect children from deadly diseases. Support UNICEF's mission for children around the world. Your tax-deductible contribution can make a difference. Donate today. TOP PHOTO: One-month-old Aline (name changed) lies in her mother’s arms at the mpox isolation unit of the UNICEF-supported Kamanyola Hospital in South Kivu province, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), on July 24, 2024. When Aline was born, a family friend who was thought to be cured from mpox visited and held her. “I saw a pimple on my daughter’s face, and I thought nothing of it at first, but as the days went by, the pimples grew in size and number,” Aline's mother says. “When we arrived at the hospital, they told me we had to be admitted ... My child had a high fever and was in a lot of pain — too much for me to bear. I urge other parents to protect their children by keeping their distances and washing their hands regularly." Time
The article was published on 2026-09-02. The 2024 outbreak data includes cases from January 2024 to mid-August 2024.
Location and population
The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) is the epicenter of the 2024 mpox outbreak. Children under age 15 represent 56 percent of mpox cases reported in the country. The disease is also spreading to neighboring countries.
Event
A new variant of mpox, or monkeypox, is killing children in Africa. The first reported human case of mpox was in 1970, in a 9-month-old boy in the DRC. The 2024 outbreak was a clade I outbreak, driven by outbreaks associated with two variants of clade I, clade Ia and clade Ib.
Scale, impact and risk
Between January 2024 and mid-August 2024, 8,772 children were infected and 463 had died. From January 1, 2022, through July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed cases of mpox and 529 deaths. Most deaths occur in children, especially those weakened by malnutrition or other conditions.
Response, status and uncertainty
The World Health Organization declared mpox a public health emergency of international concern due to its spread to several neighboring countries. UNICEF is supporting efforts by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, to stockpile mpox vaccines and strengthen outbreak response in low- and middle-income countries. The mpox vaccine is safe and highly effective at preventing severe disease.

Mpox, Covid-19 cases continue to rise - bangkokpost.com

Mpox, Covid-19 cases continue to rise - bangkokpost.com
新闻简报The Bangkok Post reported on September 2, 2026, that mpox and Covid-19 cases in Thailand have continued to rise since June 2026. From January 1 to August 23, 2026, the Department of Disease Control recorded 254 mpox cases, with 193 attributed to the Clade Ib strain and 58 to the Clade II strain. The Clade Ib strain is noted for causing more severe symptoms, particularly in individuals with low immunity. Meanwhile, from January 1 to August 31, 2026, there were approximately 99,000 Covid-19 cases, with a fatality rate of 0.02%, predominantly among those over 60 years old. No child fatalities were reported for mpox this year, attributed to sufficient medical resources and effective treatment systems. The report highlights that mpox spreads primarily through close, skin-to-skin contact, while Covid-19 spreads mainly through respiratory droplets. Prevention strategies include vaccination, maintaining personal hygiene, and avoiding close contact with infected individuals or contaminated surfaces. The situation is described as controllable, with no specific mention of a fatality rate for mpox.

时间
自2026年6月以来,特别提到2026年1月1日至8月23日的时期。
地点与对象
泰国,特别是大曼谷地区、大城市和旅游省份。
事件
New cases of mpox and Covid-19 continue to rise, with the Department of Disease Control reporting 254 mpox cases and 99,000 Covid-19 cases from Jan 1 to Aug 31, 2026.
规模、影响与风险
猴痘病例包括193例Clade Ib毒株和58例Clade II毒株。Clade Ib毒株导致更严重的症状,尤其是在免疫力较低的患者中。Covid-19的死亡率为0.02%,大多数死亡病例发生在60岁以上患者中。今年未报告猴痘导致的儿童死亡病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
泰国猴痘的情况被描述为可控。预防措施包括疫苗接种、良好的个人卫生以及避免与感染者或污染表面密切接触。传播与性接触和前往封闭、拥挤场所有关。

(File photo: Department of Disease Control New cases of monkey pox (Mpox) and coronavirus disease 2019 (Covid-19) continue to rise since June with the presence of a severer mpox strain and a low fatality rate for Covid-19, according to the Department of Disease Control. Dr Montien Kanasawat, director-general of the department, said on Wednesday that from Jan 1 to Aug 23, Thailand recorded 254 Mpox cases, including 193 cases of the Clade Ib strain and 58 cases of the Clade II strain. Patients were in Greater Bangkok, big cities and tourist provinces. Transmission resulted from sexual intercourses, which were related to visits to closed and crowded places. Dr Montien said the Mpox situation in Thailand remained controllable and the number of cases was rising for the Clade Ib strain, which caused more severe symptoms than the Clade II strain especially in patients with low immunity. Symptoms include fever, headache, swollen lymph nodes and rashes. Regarding Covid-19 infection, Dr Jurai Wongsawat, spokeswoman of the Department of Disease Control, said from Jan 1 to Aug 31 there were about 99,000 cases. Records showed that there were more cases since June due to the conditions of the wet season but total cases were lower than figures in the past three years. ADVERTISEMENT She said that the current fatality rate among Covid-19 patients was at 0.02% and most deaths were patients above the age of 60. There had not been a child fatality this year as there were enough medicine and efficient treatment systems, which limited symptoms, Dr Jurai added. Vaccination, good personal hygiene and avoiding close contact with infected people or contaminated surfaces are key to preventing both coronavirus and mpox. Covid-19 mainly spreads through respiratory droplets, whereas mpox spreads primarily through close, skin-to-skin contact. 乌克兰文尼察地区确认一例猴痘病例 - UA.NEWS

A case of monkeypox in a man has been confirmed in the Vinnytsia region - UA.NEWS
新闻简报A single case of monkeypox (mpox) in an adult male has been confirmed in the Vinnytsia region of Ukraine on 03 September 2026. The confirmation was made by the reference laboratory of the Public Health Center of the Ministry of Health of Ukraine. The case is linked to a trip to European countries where monkeypox has been reported. The patient has a mild case and is self-isolating with outpatient treatment. The Vinnytsia Regional Center for Disease Control and Prevention conducted an epidemiological investigation, identifying potential contacts who will be monitored for 21 days. The Public Health Center continues epidemiological surveillance. There are no signs of the infection spreading in Ukraine, and the virus is not transmitted through air, water, food, or casual contact. The transmission is primarily through close, direct contact with an infected person, particularly through skin areas, bodily fluids, or contaminated objects. The case is part of ongoing monitoring and does not indicate a broader outbreak at this time.

时间
该病例于2026年09月03日确认,事件日期与确认日期相同。潜伏期持续时间为5至21天,最常见的为6–16天。
地点与对象
该病例在乌克兰文尼察地区被确认,涉及一名成年男性。乌克兰卫生部公共卫生中心和文尼察地区疾病预防控制中心参与了应对工作。
事件
一名成年男性在乌克兰文尼察地区被实验室确认患有猴痘(猴痘)。确认由乌克兰卫生部公共卫生中心的参考实验室完成。
规模、影响与风险
该病例与前往欧洲国家的旅行有关,这些国家曾报告猴痘病例。目前乌克兰境内未发现感染传播迹象。患者病情较轻,正在接受门诊治疗治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
文尼察地区疾病预防控制中心开展了流行病学调查,并确定了接触者范围。这些接触者将被组织进行21天的医学监测。公共卫生中心继续进行流行病学监测工作。

Original source text (English)
News Ukraine A case of monkeypox in a man has been confirmed in the Vinnytsia region UA NEWS 03 September 2026 14:27 In the Vinnytsia region, an adult man has been laboratory-confirmed to have monkeypox (mpox). The reference laboratory of the Public Health Center of the Ministry of Health of Ukraine confirmed the diagnosis. “The reference laboratory of the Public Health Center of the Ministry of Health of Ukraine has confirmed a case of monkeypox (mpox) in an adult male in the Vinnytsia region. The case is linked to a trip to European countries where this infection has been reported in recent years. There are no signs of the infection spreading in Ukraine,” the statement reads. It is noted that genetic sequencing showed that the virus belongs to clade Ib—a variant detected in EU countries. This pathogen is not new and is well known: diagnostic tests, clinical protocols, and a vaccine are available for it. The patient has a mild case of the disease, is self-isolating, and is receiving outpatient treatment. The Vinnytsia Regional Center for Disease Control and Prevention conducted an epidemiological investigation and identified the circle of contacts who may have come into contact with the patient. Medical monitoring will be organized for these contacts for 21 days from the date of their last contact with the patient. The patient has received recommendations regarding self-isolation and personal hygiene. The Public Health Center, in collaboration with the Vinnytsia Regional Center for Disease Control and Prevention, continues epidemiological surveillance. As previously reported, the monkeypox virus is not transmitted through the air, water, food, public transportation, or casual contact with people on the street or in a store. The virus is transmitted primarily through close, direct contact with an infected person, particularly through contact with affected skin areas, bodily fluids, and contaminated objects such as bedding, towels, clothing, etc. A significant proportion of cases are associated with close physical contact, including sexual contact. 猴痘:在下一次暴发之前保护自己

Mpox: A Reminder to Protect Ourselves Before the Next Outbreak - gayexpress.co.nz
新闻简报《Gay Express》文章标题为“猴痘:在下一次暴发之前保护自己”,其内容为一般性的公共卫生提醒,而非具体描述猴痘事件。文章强调在任何未来猴痘可能再次出现之前,个人防护措施和保持警惕的重要性,但并未提供任何关于确诊病例、死亡或暴露的数据,也未描述正在进行的调查、检测或官方应对活动。因此,该文章作为对社区的一般性指导,未涉及具体的暴发或流行病学情况。
查看新闻五要素
时间

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

全球猴痘 疫苗储备库向所有国家开放 - Devdiscourse

Global Mpox Vaccine Stockpile Opens Access to Every Country - Devdiscourse
新闻简报A new global mpox vaccine stockpile was launched on 27 August 2026, providing every country with a shared route to request doses during outbreaks, regardless of income or purchasing power. The initiative, approved by Gavi in 2025, aims to address previous disparities in vaccine access. Between 1 January 2022 and 31 July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed mpox cases and 529 deaths, with two-thirds of infections occurring in the WHO African Region. Mpox spreads through close physical contact and remains a threat, particularly to children and immunocompromised individuals. The stockpile is expected to begin operations later this month, offering a permanent emergency vaccine source to prevent access gaps and support outbreak control. The mechanism is part of lessons learned from the 2024 mpox Public Health Emergency of International Concern, emphasizing the need for a fairer system in vaccine distribution.
查看新闻五要素
时间
Between 1 January 2022 and 31 July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed mpox cases and 529 deaths, with around two-thirds of reported infections occurring in the WHO African Region. The new global stockpile was launched on 27 August 2026 and is expected to begin operations later this month.
地点与对象
145个国家和领土,其中约三分之二的报告感染病例发生于世界卫生组织非洲地区。该储备库向所有国家开放。
事件
一项新的全球猴痘 疫苗储备库于2026年8月27日启动,为所有国家在暴发期间提供共享途径以请求剂量,无论其收入水平或购买力。该储备库于2025年获得Gavi董事会批准,并由Gavi资助。
规模、影响与风险
2022年1月1日至2026年7月31日期间,全球共报告了190,683例确认的猴痘病例和529例死亡。猴痘可引起疼痛性皮肤病变、发热和其他严重症状,儿童和免疫功能低下者面临更高的严重疾病风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该储备库预计将在本月晚些时候开始运作,创建一个永久性的紧急疫苗支持来源。其设计目的是防止获取差距,确保基于公共卫生需求的公平分配。

Global Mpox Vaccine Stockpile Opens Access to Every Country - Devdiscourse

News Brief
A new global mpox vaccine stockpile was launched on 27 August 2026, providing every country with a shared route to request doses during outbreaks, regardless of income or purchasing power. The initiative, approved by Gavi in 2025, aims to address previous disparities in vaccine access. Between 1 January 2022 and 31 July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed mpox cases and 529 deaths, with two-thirds of infections occurring in the WHO African Region. Mpox spreads through close physical contact and remains a threat, particularly to children and immunocompromised individuals. The stockpile is expected to begin operations later this month, offering a permanent emergency vaccine source to prevent access gaps and support outbreak control. The mechanism is part of lessons learned from the 2024 mpox Public Health Emergency of International Concern, emphasizing the need for a fairer system in vaccine distribution.
Original source text (English)
About Career Advertisement Media Partnership Contact Us HOME NEWS RESEARCH LIVE DISCOURSE PERSPECTIVES AND INSIGHTS SUBMIT PRESS RELEASE NEWS DESK RESEARCH LIVE DISCOURSE PERSPECTIVES AND INSIGHTS INTERVIEW Agro-Forestry Art & Culture Technology Economy Education Energy Politics Law & Governance Health Science Social Sports Transport Urban Development WASH Home News Health Article Global Mpox Vaccine Stockpile Opens Access to Every Country Between 1 January 2022 and 31 July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed mpox cases and 529 deaths, with around two-thirds of reported infections occurring in the WHO African Region. Devdiscourse News Desk | Geneva | Updated: 03-09-2026 12:25 IST | Created: 03-09-2026 12:25 IST Representative image Image Credit: ChatGPT SHARE Key Takeaways AI Summary Analyzing article... A new global stockpile of mpox vaccines was launched on 27 August, giving every country a shared route to request doses during an outbreak, regardless of its income or purchasing power. The mechanism is expected to begin operations later this month, creating a permanent source of emergency vaccine support designed to prevent the access gaps that left many lower-income countries waiting for protection during earlier waves of the disease. A Fairer System for Emergency Vaccine Access The stockpile was approved by the Board of Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance, in 2025 and is being financed by Gavi, while vaccine allocation will be coordinated by the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision. The group brings together the International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies, Médecins Sans Frontières, UNICEF and the World Health Organization, combining vaccine procurement, public health knowledge, humanitarian delivery networks and experience working directly with communities affected by disease outbreaks. The initiative grows from the Access and Allocation Mechanism created during the 2024 mpox Public Health Emergency of International Concern, when the need for faster and fairer vaccine distribution became clear. Countries facing outbreaks will now have a defined global channel through which they can seek doses based on public health need and their potential effect on controlling transmission, rather than competing in a market where wealthier buyers can secure supplies first. Mpox Remains a Threat Across the World Between 1 January 2022 and 31 July 2026, 145 countries and territories reported 190,683 confirmed mpox cases and 529 deaths, with around two-thirds of reported infections occurring in the WHO African Region. The virus spreads mainly through close physical contact, while transmission from animals to people remains a concern in parts of Africa and continues to create conditions in which fresh outbreaks can emerge. Mpox can cause painful skin lesions, fever and other serious symptoms, with children and people who have weakened immune systems facing a greater risk of severe illness. Vaccination can protect people who are most exposed, reduce the chances of serious disease and help health teams bring outbreaks under control before infections spread into more communities. UNICEF Executive Director Catherine Russell also drew attention to the wider effect on children, including stigma, interrupted education, fear and the emotional strain caused by separation from relatives and friends. Partners Call the Stockpile a Lifesaving Step Gavi CEO Dr Sania Nishtar said the unequal vaccine access seen during the 2022 outbreak showed why a global mechanism was needed, stressing that the ability to pay should not decide which countries can use vaccines to protect lives. IFRC Secretary General and CEO Jagan Chapagain described universal access as vital, saying coordinated distribution can help doses reach communities facing the greatest danger at the time they are needed. MSF International Medical Coordinator Dr Daniela Garone said the presence of different mpox variants affecting different populations requires close cooperation among national authorities, regional bodies, disease programmes, health managers and local communities. WHO Director-General Dr Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus called the stockpile an important move towards a more sustainable and equitable outbreak response, noting that quicker vaccine access can save lives and contain transmission before it expands. The initiative turns lessons from recent mpox emergencies into a standing system that countries can rely on when the next outbreak places communities at risk. 全球倡议旨在扩大对猴痘 疫苗在暴发中的获取 - THISDAYLIVE

Global Initiative Targets Wider Access to Mpox Vaccines in Outbreak Response - THISDAYLIVE
新闻简报一项全球性倡议已启动,以改善对猴痘 疫苗的获取,并确保在暴发期间实现公平分配。该倡议基于世界卫生组织(WHO)及其合作伙伴的工作,包括于1997年成立的国际疫苗供应协调小组(ICG),旨在协调疫苗和抗生素的紧急供应以应对疾病暴发。ICG现在将管理猴痘 疫苗的储备,建立在2024年引入的获取与分配机制(AAM)基础上。目标是减少未来猴痘 暴发响应中的延迟,并确保受影响最严重的国家不会在获取疫苗方面被落下。该倡议补充了国家公共卫生措施,并旨在解决疫苗供应、资金、配送和公平获取方面的挑战。2025年,Gavi董事会在ICG框架下批准建立了全球猴痘 疫苗储备。这一举措反映了从短期应急安排向更可持续的疫苗准备系统的转变。

时间
该倡议于2026年启动,文章于2026-09-03发布。获取与分配机制(AAM)于2024年建立,Gavi董事会于2025年批准了储备。

该倡议具有全球性,关注受猴痘 暴发影响的国家,特别是那些获取疫苗受限的国家。它涉及国际合作伙伴、捐助者和疫苗制造商。
事件
一项全球性倡议已启动,以改善对猴痘 疫苗的获取,并确保在暴发期间实现公平分配。该倡议基于世界卫生组织(WHO)及其合作伙伴的工作,包括国际疫苗供应协调小组(ICG)。
规模、影响与风险
该倡议旨在减少未来猴痘 暴发响应中的延迟,并确保受影响最严重的国家不会在获取疫苗方面被落下。预计该倡议将补充国家公共卫生措施,并解决疫苗供应、资金、配送和公平获取方面的挑战。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Global Initiative Targets Wider Access to Mpox Vaccines in Outbreak Response - THISDAYLIVE

News Brief
A global initiative has been launched to improve access to mpox vaccines and ensure equitable distribution during outbreaks. The initiative, based on the work of the World Health Organisation (WHO) and its partners, includes the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision (ICG), established in 1997 to coordinate emergency supplies of vaccines and antibiotics for disease outbreaks. The ICG will now manage mpox vaccine stockpiles, building on the Access and Allocation Mechanism (AAM) introduced in 2024. The goal is to reduce delays in responding to future mpox outbreaks and ensure that countries most affected are not left behind in accessing vaccines. The initiative complements national public health measures and aims to address challenges in vaccine supply, financing, delivery, and equitable access. The Gavi Board approved the establishment of a global mpox vaccine stockpile in 2025 under the ICG framework. This move reflects a shift from short-term emergency arrangements to a more sustainable system for vaccine preparedness.
Original source text (English)
Menu Menu Home Business Politics Nigeria International 2Open submenu Health & Wellbeing Education Life & Style Sport Editorial Backpage Close submenuInternational Africa Rest of the World Sunday, 6th September, 2026 Home Business Politics Nigeria International Africa Rest of the World Health & Wellbeing Education Life & Style Sport Editorial Backpage Search for: Breaking News Atiku, Osinbajo, Mark, Others Attend Amaechi Mother’s Burial 1 day ago Wike: I’ll Be Atiku, Obi’s Main Target If Tinubu Loses 2027 Polls 4 days ago Aliko Dangote: FG’s Reforms Driving Nigeria’s Economic Recovery, Restoring Investors’ Confidence 4 days ago Fidelity Bank Opens SME Hub, Offers Free Training to 1,000 Entrepreneurs in Kano 4 days ago Latest Headlines Sunbeth Redeems ₦25.1bn CP, Demonstrates Strong Liquidity, Investor Confidence Nigeria Escorts Koroma Home as Two-Year Political Exile Ends BNXN, Runtown, Patoranking To Storm New York For Nigeria’s 66th Independence Day Parade Adeleke’s CoS, Akinleye, Empowers Pardoned Inmates With POS Machines, Cash Global Initiative Targets Wider Access to Mpox Vaccines in Outbreak Response Nigeria | 4 days ago Shares Share Post Email Share Share Ayodeji Ake A new global initiative has been launched to improve access to mpox vaccines and ensure that countries affected by outbreaks can receive vaccines more quickly and equitably when they are needed. InfectiousDiseases The initiative builds on the work of the World Health Organisation (WHO) and its partners to strengthen international coordination of vaccine supplies and improve preparedness for mpox outbreaks, particularly in countries with limited access to vaccines. At the centre of the initiative is the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision (ICG), a mechanism established in 1997 following major meningitis outbreaks in Africa. ICG was created to coordinate emergency supplies of vaccines and antibiotics for countries facing major disease outbreaks. Its role is based on the recognition that although outbreaks are difficult to predict, timely vaccination can help prevent further transmission and reduce the impact of infectious diseases. Traditionally, ICG has been responsible for ensuring equitable access to licensed vaccines against cholera, meningitis, yellow fever, and Ebola virus disease during emergencies. Its responsibilities include managing global emergency vaccine stockpiles and working with partners, donors, and vaccine manufacturers to determine the size and composition of the stocks required for effective outbreak response. The expansion of the mechanism to include mpox is expected to provide a more structured approach to the distribution of mpox vaccines, particularly during emergencies when demand can rise rapidly. The move builds on measures introduced during the global mpox response. In 2024, WHO and its partners established an Access and Allocation Mechanism (AAM) specifically for mpox vaccines, treatments and diagnostic tests. AAM was designed to support the allocation of limited medical countermeasures to countries and communities where they were most urgently required. Under the new, longer-term arrangement, ICG will continue and strengthen the work initiated through the temporary mechanism. The development also follows a decision by the Gavi Board in 2025 to formally approve the establishment and financing of a global mpox vaccine stockpile under the long-standing ICG framework. InfectiousDiseases The stockpile is intended to ensure that vaccine supplies can be mobilised rapidly during outbreaks instead of relying solely on vaccine procurement after an emergency has already escalated. The initiative comes amid continued concern over mpox, a viral disease that can cause fever, rash, swollen lymph nodes and painful lesions. While many people recover, severe disease and death can occur, particularly among vulnerable populations. WHO has stressed the importance of vaccination as part of a broader outbreak response that also includes surveillance, testing, case management, contact tracing, infection prevention and community engagement. The global vaccine initiative is expected to complement, rather than replace, national public health measures. Health authorities and international partners will also need to address challenges around vaccine supply, financing, delivery and equitable access to ensure that the availability of a global stockpile translates into timely vaccination on the ground. The expansion of the ICG mechanism to include mpox represents a shift from short-term emergency arrangements towards a more sustainable system for vaccine preparedness. By maintaining emergency stocks and establishing clearer mechanisms for allocation, global health agencies aim to reduce delays in responding to future mpox outbreaks and ensure that countries most affected are not left behind in accessing lifesaving vaccines. Event
A global initiative was launched to improve access to mpox vaccines and ensure equitable distribution during outbreaks. The initiative is based on the work of the World Health Organisation (WHO) and its partners, including the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision (ICG).
Scale, impact and risk
The initiative aims to reduce delays in responding to future mpox outbreaks and ensure that countries most affected are not left behind in accessing vaccines. Response, status and uncertainty

The ICG will continue and strengthen the work initiated through the temporary Access and Allocation Mechanism (AAM).

英国确认新猴痘变种4例病例,首次在非洲以外地区出现 - Arab News PK

UK identifies 4 cases of new mpox variant, the first cluster outside Africa - Arab News PK
新闻简报英国卫生安全局已确认新猴痘变种的4例病例,标志着该变种首次在非洲以外地区出现。该变种最早于今年年初在刚果东部被发现,已在包括刚果、布隆迪、肯尼亚、卢旺达和乌干达在内的多个非洲国家引发暴发疫情,累计有超过43,000例疑似病例和超过1,000例死亡,主要集中在刚果。科学家认为该变种引起的症状较轻且不易察觉,因此更容易传播。英国的病例涉及同一家庭居住的个体,本周又发现了3例新增病例。世界卫生组织于2024年8月因该变种在非洲的传播宣布了全球紧急状态,并已向9个非洲国家分配了899,900剂疫苗疫苗。根据卫生官员的说法,公众的风险仍然较低。
查看新闻五要素
时间
英国卫生安全局于上周宣布在英国发现新猴痘变种的首例病例,并于本周确认了另外3例病例。世界卫生组织于2024年8月宣布了全球紧急状态,本文于2026年9月4日发表。
地点与对象
病例在英国被发现,具体位于伦敦,涉及同一家庭居住的个体。该暴发疫情也报告出现在多个非洲国家,包括刚果、布隆迪、肯尼亚、卢旺达和乌干达,非洲地区累计有超过43,000例疑似病例和超过1,000例死亡。
事件
英国卫生安全局确认新猴痘变种的4例病例,这是该变种首次在非洲以外地区出现。该变种最早于今年年初在刚果东部被发现,并已在多个非洲国家引发暴发疫情。科学家认为该变种引起较轻的症状且不易察觉,因此更容易传播。
规模、影响与风险
该新变种已在多个非洲国家引发暴发疫情,累计有约43,000例疑似病例和超过1,000例死亡,主要集中在刚果。英国已确认4例病例,其中3例为同一家庭居住的个体。科学家表示公众的风险仍然较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
英国卫生安全局正在监测这些病例,并在伦敦医院对患者进行治疗。世界卫生组织已向9个非洲国家分配了899,900剂疫苗疫苗。该变种在非洲的传播引发了全球紧急状态的宣布,且公众的风险被评估为较低。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

UK identifies 4 cases of new mpox variant, the first cluster outside Africa - Arab News PK

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency has identified four cases of a new mpox variant, marking the first cluster outside Africa. The variant, first detected in eastern Congo earlier this year, has caused outbreaks in several African countries, including Congo, Burundi, Kenya, Rwanda, and Uganda, with over 43,000 suspect cases and more than 1,000 deaths, mostly in Congo. Scientists believe the variant causes milder symptoms that are harder to notice, making it easier to spread. The UK cases involve individuals who lived in the same household, with three further cases identified this week. The World Health Organization declared a global emergency in August 2024 due to the spread of the variant in Africa and has allocated 899,900 vaccine doses to nine African countries. The risk to the public remains low, according to health officials.

The virus particles are in various stages of maturity, which accounts for differences in shape. (AP/File) Short Url https://arab.news/vsrey Scientists believe it causes milder symptoms that are harder to notice, which makes it easier to spread because people may not know they are infected Updated 7 November 2024 AP November 7, 2024 01:04 LONDON: British health officials say they have identified four cases of the new, more infectious version of mpox that first emerged in Congo, marking the first time the variant has caused a cluster of illness outside of Africa. Scientists said the risk to the public remains low. Authorities announced the first case of the new form of mpox in the UK last week, saying the case was being treated at a London hospital after recently traveling to countries in Africa with ongoing outbreaks. This week, the UK Health Security Agency said it had now identified three further cases who lived in the same household as the first patient. They too are now being treated at a hospital in London. “Mpox is very infectious in households with close contact and so it is not unexpected to see further cases within the same household,” said Susan Hopkins, chief medical adviser of the UK Health Security Agency. The new variant of mpox was first detected earlier this year in eastern Congo. Scientists believe it causes milder symptoms that are harder to notice, which makes it easier to spread because people may not know they are infected. Its spread in Congo and elsewhere in Africa prompted the World Health Organization to declare a global emergency in August. Britain recorded more than 3,000 cases of another type of mpox during a 2022 outbreak that hit more than 100 countries. The new variant of mpox has also caused outbreaks in Burundi, Kenya, Rwanda and Uganda. Single cases in travelers have also been reported in Sweden, India, Germany and Thailand. To date, there have been about 43,000 suspect cases of mpox in Africa, including more than 1,000 deaths, mostly in Congo. On Wednesday, WHO said it had allocated 899,900 vaccine doses to nine African countries struggling with mpox epidemics. Time
The UK Health Security Agency announced the first case of the new mpox variant in the UK last week, and identified three further cases this week. The WHO declared a global emergency in August 2024, and the article was published on September 4, 2026.
Location and population
The cases were identified in the UK, specifically in London, and involve individuals who lived in the same household. The outbreak is also reported in several African countries, including Congo, Burundi, Kenya, Rwanda, and Uganda, with over 43,000 suspect cases and more than 1,000 deaths in Africa.
Event
The UK Health Security Agency identified four cases of a new mpox variant, the first cluster outside Africa. The variant was first detected in eastern Congo earlier this year and has caused outbreaks in several African countries. Scientists believe it causes milder symptoms that are harder to notice, making it easier to spread.
Scale, impact and risk
The new variant has caused outbreaks in multiple African countries, with about 43,000 suspect cases and over 1,000 deaths, mostly in Congo. The UK has identified four cases, with three further cases in the same household. Scientists stated the risk to the public remains low.
Response, status and uncertainty
The UK Health Security Agency is monitoring the cases and treating them at a London hospital. The WHO has allocated 899,900 vaccine doses to nine African countries. The spread of the variant in Africa prompted a global emergency declaration, and the risk to the public is considered low.

全球疫苗试验即将开始,因埃博拉和猴痘在非洲传播 - thenationalnews.com

Global vaccine trials to begin as Ebola and mpox spread in Africa - thenationalnews.com
新闻简报本文报道了全球对埃博拉和猴痘在非洲传播的应对情况。世界卫生组织(WHO)于2026年5月15日宣布在刚果民主共和国(DRC)爆发的埃博拉暴发疫情,该疫情已发展为该国历史上最严重的一次,造成3,007人死亡,确诊6,186例。WHO正在协调针对刚果民主共和国Bundibugyo株埃博拉的疫苗试验。与此同时,由Gavi资助的猴痘 疫苗储备计划于2026年8月27日启动,旨在确保低收入国家能够公平获得疫苗。相较于埃博拉,猴痘的危险性较低,已传播至全球145个国家,其中DRC和一些欧洲国家的Clade Ib株导致病例增加。阿联酋对记录有埃博拉病例的非洲国家实施了旅行限制,并此前向五个非洲国家空运了疫苗和医疗物资。WHO警告称,埃博拉暴发对DRC、其邻国以及更广泛的地区构成重大威胁,强调需要协调应对以切断传播的传播链。
查看新闻五要素
时间
世界卫生组织(WHO)于2026年5月15日正式宣布刚果民主共和国(DRC)爆发埃博拉暴发疫情。阿联酋对记录有埃博拉病例的非洲国家实施了旅行限制。猴痘 疫苗储备计划于2026年8月27日启动。本文于2026年9月3日发表。
地点与对象
埃博拉暴发疫情发生在刚果民主共和国(DRC),具体位于Ituri省。猴痘 暴发已传播至全球约145个国家,DRC和一些欧洲国家报告了病例。
事件
刚果民主共和国(DRC)埃博拉死亡人数达到3,007人,确诊6,186例。全球针对Bundibugyo株埃博拉的疫苗试验预计将在下月开始。由Gavi资助的猴痘 疫苗储备计划于2026年8月27日启动,旨在确保低收入国家能够公平获得疫苗。
规模、影响与风险
The DRC Ebola outbreak has reached 3,007 deaths and 6,186 confirmed cases, making it the worst in the country's history. Mpox has caused 190,683 cases and 529 deaths worldwide, with the Clade Ib strain increasing cases in the DRC and some European countries. The fatality rate for mpox is about 10%, while for Ebola it ranges from 25 to 90%. The WHO warned that the Ebola outbreak poses a threat to DRC, its neighbors, and the wider region.
应对、状态与不确定性
WHO正在协调受影响地区的各项努力。阿联酋对记录有埃博拉病例的非洲国家实施了旅行限制,并此前向五个非洲国家空运了疫苗和医疗物资。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Global vaccine trials to begin as Ebola and mpox spread in Africa - thenationalnews.com

News Brief
The article reports on the global response to the spread of Ebola and mpox in Africa. The WHO declared the Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) on May 15, 2026, and it has since become the country's worst ever, with 3,007 deaths and 6,186 confirmed cases. The Bundibugyo strain of Ebola is the focus of upcoming vaccine trials, coordinated by the WHO. Meanwhile, an mpox vaccine stockpile initiative, funded by Gavi, was launched on August 27, 2026, to ensure equitable access to vaccines for lower-income countries. Mpox, which is less dangerous than Ebola, has spread to 145 countries globally, with the Clade Ib strain causing an increase in cases in the DRC and some European nations. The UAE has imposed travel restrictions to and from African countries with Ebola cases and has previously provided vaccines and medical supplies to five African nations. The WHO warned that the Ebola outbreak poses a significant threat to the DRC, its neighbors, and the wider region, emphasizing the need for a coordinated response to break the chain of transmission.
Original source text (English)

Global vaccine trials to begin as Ebola and mpox spread in Africa DRC Ebola death toll reaches 3,007 as WHO chief warns of threat to wider region Nick Webster September 03, 2026 Add on Google The DR Congo Ebola outbreak has become the country's worst ever, with 3,007 deaths and 6,186 confirmed cases recorded. Global vaccine trials for Ebola's Bundibugyo strain are expected as early as next month, with the WHO coordinating efforts across affected regions. An mpox vaccine stockpile initiative, funded by Gavi, launched on August 27 to ensure lower-income countries have equitable access to shots. The UAE has restricted travel to and from African nations recording Ebola cases and previously airlifted vaccines and medical supplies to five African countries. "During the 2022 mpox outbreak, lower-income countries were left behind when it came to access to vaccines," said Gavi chief executive Dr Sania Nishtar. The Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo is now the worst in the country's history, with the death toll having reached 3,007 amid an international push to develop vaccines to curb the spread of the disease. At least 6,186 cases have been confirmed in the DR Congo, with infections also spreading across its porous borders. Patients have contracted the Bundibugyo strain of the disease and the World Health Organisation said potential vaccines were to be tested to help tackle the outbreak. Trials could take place next month or in November, with other treatments also to be considered, it added. "Until every chain is found and broken, the epidemic will continue and will continue to pose a threat to DRC, its neighbours and the region as a whole," said WHO director general Dr Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus. DRC Ebola cases surpass 4,000 as officials warn of fastest spreading outbreak on record Read More The WHO formally declared the outbreak in the DRC's Ituri province on May 15. Cases have since been reported in six nearby areas. It is the worst Ebola crisis since the West African outbreak between 2014 and 2016, in which at least 28,600 people were infected and more than 11,000 died in Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone. Travel restrictions The scale of the crisis was underlined this summer when travellers showing symptoms of Ebola were admitted to hospital in Israel after arriving from the DRC. Test results came back negative, but the incident highlighted the risks posed by international travel from areas where infections are spreading. The UAE imposed the precautionary measure of restricting travel to and from African countries that have recorded Ebola cases. Health workers bury a patient who died from Ebola in Ituri province, in the DRC. EPA Show caption: Health workers bury a patient who died from Ebola in Ituri p... Mpox outbreak Alongside the Ebola crisis, health authorities have also taken steps to improve access to mpox vaccines. An outbreak of the disease, which began in 2022, has spread to about 145 countries, with 190,683 cases and 529 deaths recorded worldwide. The Clade Ib strain has led to an increase in cases across the DRC, as well as some European countries. Mpox is much less dangerous than Ebola. About 10 per cent of cases proves deadly, compared with a fatality rate of 25 to 90 per cent for Ebola, depending on access to treatment. Both Ebola and mpox are spread differently. Mpox in transferred through close skin-to-skin contact, while Ebola is spread through contact with blood, bodily fluids or contaminated surfaces. Global vaccine stockpile A global initiative to increase a stockpile of mpox vaccines was launched on August 27, aiming to develop a long-term system to ensure countries have access to shots. It has been funded by Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance in co-ordination with the International Co-ordinating Group on Vaccine Provision. “During the 2022 mpox outbreak, lower-income countries were left behind when it came to access to vaccines,” said Dr Sania Nishtar, chief executive of Gavi. “It was clear that a mechanism was needed to ensure this global public good could be accessed by those who need it most. Our hope is that this stockpile will keep the world safer from the threat of this deadly disease.” In 2024, the UAE played a vital role in funding and distributing vaccines to African nations tackling mpox and Ebola cases. Shots and other medical support were flown to the DRC, Nigeria, South Africa, Ivory Coast and Cameroon. "Support is particularly important for childhood vaccination programmes," said Unicef executive director Catherine Russell. “Mpox can be devastating for children, not just its physical effects, but also the stigma, interrupted learning and fear that can come with being isolated from family and friends."
